# Architecture Review — 2026-09-02

**Status:** live. **Date:** 2026-09-02 (written 2026-09-03). **Compiler:** Earendil.
**Tree the findings were re-derived against:** `main` @ `532463ae35` (Batch 1390), working tree as found.

## 1. Purpose and the standing rule

This file is the **architecture-review authority** for the CesiumJS WebGPU fork. It carries the live findings of ten retired reviews, re-derived at today's code by a second reader, in the corrected wording, with the current `file:line` and the queue row that owns each one.

Five authorities are unchanged and must be **linked, never duplicated**:

| Authority | Owns |
| --- | --- |
| [`FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN_2026-07-13.md`](FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN_2026-07-13.md) | The **remediation** authority — FAR packages, waves, gates, ADR-1..ADR-8 |
| [`FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_LEDGER_2026-07-13.json`](FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_LEDGER_2026-07-13.json) | FAR package state (`findings{}`, `state`, `gate`) |
| [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) | The **reference** — how the fork is built, layer by layer |
| [`PICKING_ARCHITECTURE_STATE_2026-08-17.md`](PICKING_ARCHITECTURE_STATE_2026-08-17.md) | Picking state (the `LD-` ids) |
| [`CLOUD_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md`](CLOUD_ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md) | Planetary-cloud correctness, temporal, weather, quality, performance |

**The standing rule.** This file states _what is true about the architecture and where_. It does not schedule work, does not carry status, and does not override a queue row. The campaign queues, [`DEFERRED_WORK.md`](DEFERRED_WORK.md) and [`FIX_QUEUE_2026-08-27_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md`](FIX_QUEUE_2026-08-27_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md) remain the sole status authorities for every id they own. Where a finding below says **"unowned"**, that is itself the finding: nothing in the tracked tree will schedule it until a row exists.

**Evidence bank.** Every claim traces to [`audits/2026-09-02_old_review_validity/`](audits/2026-09-02_old_review_validity/) — ten `*.verified.md` second-reader adjudications, `SUMMARY.md`, `INBOUND_REFERENCES.md`, and the ten `*.judgement.json` first-reader files. **Take text from the `.verified.md` files, never from the `.judgement.json` files:** 57 of 236 open items needed correction, so the raw judgements carry 57 known-wrong premises. The judgement files are retained permanently because they are the only surviving record of the first reading of the 440 closed items; batch L3 (2026-09-04) re-derived the 375 of them no verifier had reached (§7.1).

## 2. Supersession table

Nothing was deleted. The seven documents below are **archived**, not removed: their full text stays readable at the archive path. Three more came back KEEP-PART from their verifier and **stay live where they are**; they are listed under the main table.

Archive path convention: the repository's existing `migration_doc/archive/` layout — root-level documents move to `archive/<basename>`, the 2026-04-30 trio to `archive/audits-2026-04-30/`, and the renderer-deep review joins its two companion pillars in `archive/principal-review-2026-04-16/`. **The move landed 2026-09-03**; the moved files are byte-identical apart from relative links re-resolved for their new depth, so nothing inside them was rewritten. ## 3. Migrated findings, by subsystem

Every **CONFIRMED** and **CORRECTED** item from the ten verified banks, in the corrected wording, grouped by subsystem rather than by source document. 231 items: 174 CONFIRMED (still true as first judged) and 57 CORRECTED (real, but the first reader's scope, owner, severity or number was wrong).

Column key. **id** carries its source register prefix, so an id resolves through §5 and through the bank. **Verdict** is the second reader's. **Owner** is a queue/ledger row verified present on 2026-09-03; **unowned** means nothing in the tracked tree schedules it. **Source** names the verified bank file the text came from.

Five items were **REFUTED** and must not be migrated; two more were corrected to closed. They are recorded once in §3.16 so nobody re-derives them a third time.

### 3.1 Globe, terrain & imagery

| id | Finding (corrected wording) | Verdict | Current `file:line` | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `C.6` | `WebGPUVideoTextureManager`'s `texture_external` path has no consumer. The module has zero consumers outside its own file; its header names the live alternative (`WebGPUImageUpload` per-frame `copyExternalImageToTexture`). **Caution for the reader:** the fork-vs-upstream `ADOPT-MATRIX` row calls video "RESOLVED" — true of the manager's internals, false of the gap. `G5-tiles-model-parity.md:802-804` lists the file in the Principle-7 do-not-delete set, so "schedule its removal" is not a free option; consume-or-delete needs an explicit decision either way. | CONFIRMED | `Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUVideoTextureManager.ts:1-42` (self-declared ORPHANED docstring; `importExternalTexture` at `:171`, `:201`); `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:567` tags it ORPHANED | **unowned** — dossier only at `campaign11_planning/guides/G5-tiles-model-parity.md:758-772` |
| `R1` (feature-inventory) | Globe renderer size **and** the FEAT-GAP-09 LUT rollout count. `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts` is 3,080 lines (not 3,933) and a DX row **does** cover it. Separately, 34 of 60 `Shaders/WebGPU/Primitive/*.wgsl` live-consume the atmosphere LUT (branching on `effects.atmosphereLutControl.x > 0.5`) against the inventory's "12 of ~44 … ~32 remaining", which still lists shaders that are wired today. | `H17` | Globe material data is repacked and uploaded **per visible tile**, not once per frame per material type — see §4 R3, this is a reversal of a credited fix. `_getOrCreateMaterialPipeline` is invoked inside `createTileCommands`, which Scene calls once per tile; each invocation runs `packMaterialUBO` (a fresh `ArrayBuffer` + `Float32Array`) and `device.queue.writeBuffer`. Only the pipeline and shader-module cache entry is per `material.type`. Thousands of redundant allocations and queue writes per frame on a material-enabled globe. **Scope note:** inert unless `globe.material` is set, which is not the default. | CORRECTED (worse than judged) | `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:522` (defn), `:1625` (call), `:1099` (`createTileCommands`), `:650-654` (pack + write), `:543-588` (per-type cache); `Scene/GlobeSurfaceTileProviderRendering.js:1356`; `WebGPUGlobeMaterial.ts:396-397` | FAR-303 |
| `HP-05` | Persistent resource realization is reachable **during draw-command emission**: the globe calls `getOrCreateTileBuffersHelper` from command construction, and a miss destroys the stale entry and creates/uploads new vertex and index buffers (up-converting Uint8 indices) inside the emit path. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:1317`, `:2530`, `:2716`; `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTileBuffers.ts:177-235` | FAR-209 / FAR-210 (rows live in the **closed** CAMPAIGN8/9 queues — re-home before quoting as ownership) |
| `HP-07` | Imagery reprojection and mip generation each fragment the frame's submissions with their own `device.queue.submit([encoder.finish()])`. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUImageryReprojection.ts:245`; `WebGPUMipmapGenerator.ts:439` | FAR-200 S2 (C9 item 45) + FAR-402 (**no row**) |
| `H24` | Device pooling does not yet imply safe resource sharing — **corrected and narrowed.** Drop the sub-claim that `model._webgpuCache` can serve the wrong device: `disposeStaleWebGPUModelCache` drains any cache whose `device` or `resourceGeneration` differs and installs an `onDeviceInvalidated` subscription. What remains true: terrain residency carries **no provider or content-hash identity** — only tile coordinates plus a `sourceVertices` array-reference check — and terrain is never retired below the device lifetime because eviction runs only at teardown. | CORRECTED | key `` `${tile.level}_${tile.x}_${tile.y}` `` at `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTileBuffers.ts:174`, identity check `:198-200`; `evictStaleResources` `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:2813`, sole non-export caller `:2971` with `new Set()`; `WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:5287-5300`, `:5302-5325` | C9-15 / FAR-203 / FAR-207 / FAR-208 |
| `F5-02` / `ACTION-5` | Terrain-buffer lifetime is decoupled from the quadtree LRU. | `GLOBE-1` | Transparent Web-Mercator imagery renders opaque on WebGPU, per tile. The reproject FS forces `alpha = 1.0` (`Shaders/WebGPU/ReprojectWebMercator.wgsl:103`, limitation comment `:94-102`; WebGL keeps alpha at `Shaders/ReprojectWebMercatorFS.glsl:63`); every Mercator provider routes through it (`Scene/ImageryLayer.js:597-600`, `:624-632`), while `useWebMercatorT` tiles bind the alpha-preserving direct texture (`WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTextures.ts:335-368`), so one layer is opaque on some tiles and translucent on others in one frame. Lead for the fixer (verifier, not asserted): the shader's stated `copyExternalImageToTexture` premise looks stale — the direct texture uploads by the same call and keeps alpha — so test a straight pass-through before writing a source-format conditional. | DEFECT | as cited; `WebGPUImageryReprojection.ts:387-391` | **unowned** (doc caveats only: `IMAGERY_PROJECTION.md:93`, `WEBGPU_DEBUGGING_LOG.md:9059`) |

| `GLOBE-11` | **The WebGPU globe tests its clipping planes in the wrong coordinate frame**, and it is not alone. `Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:4463` calls `globeClipByPlanes(input.v_positionMC)` where `:1501` sets `v_positionMC = position3DWC` -- an **absolute ECEF** world position (`:1427` `let position3DWC = exaggeratedPosition + center3D;`) -- against a plane equation packed in **eye space** (`WebGPUClippingPlaneCollection.ts:161-167`). The verifier enumerated **all six** shipped `clippingPlaneTex` consumers: three consume it in the frame it is packed in (`Shaders/WebGPU/Model/ModelPBRComplete.wgsl:2312`, `Shaders/WebGPU/Primitive/PrimitivePhongColor.wgsl:439`, `PrimitivePhongTexturedColor.wgsl:412`); the globe does not. **The pack is not the odd one out -- the fix goes on the consumer**, and `v_positionEC` already exists in the same shader (`:787` varying, `:1484` assignment from `camera.modifiedModelView`), so it is one argument at `:4463` and one at `:4474`. **Widened: three consumers are wrong, not one — and they do not fail equally.** The **globe** (`Shaders/WebGPU/Globe/GlobeTerrain.wgsl:4463`, `:4474`) passes an **absolute ECEF** position (`:1501` `v_positionMC = position3DWC`, from `:1427` `exaggeratedPosition + center3D`) against an eye-space plane. That is precisely the regime `WebGPUClippingPlaneCollection.ts:161-167` warns about — "`distance` has components of order 1e7, eyePos is near 0, and the test collapses to `sign(distance)` (clips everything or nothing)" — so the globe leg is a **collapse**: the whole surface is clipped or none of it is. The two primitive shaders (`Shaders/WebGPU/Primitive/PrimitiveBasicColor.wgsl:164`, `PrimitiveDepthFailColor.wgsl:144`) pass a **camera-relative world-axis** vector — their VS sets `eyePosition` from `translateRelativeToEye(...)` (`:120` / `:107`, fn `:107-112` / `:94-99`) with no rotation into eye axes — so their error is a **pure rotation**: the clip plane is mis-oriented by the view rotation, a wrong cut rather than a degenerate one. Same defect *kind*, very different blast radius: **scope, schedule and measure the globe leg separately from the two primitive legs, and do not read "the same wrong-frame clip" as "the same severity".** The one-argument fix at `:4463`/`:4474` is the globe's (`v_positionEC` already exists at `:787`/`:1484`); the primitive legs need the same substitution their phong siblings already make (`PrimitivePhongColor.wgsl:230` computes `viewPosition = (camera.modelViewRelativeToEye * eyePos).xyz` and `:439` clips against it). A globe-only fix leaves two shipped shaders wrong. | DEFECT | as cited | **unowned** |

| `TILES-6` | **WebGPU vector-tile picking diverges from WebGL in two ways on the same pipeline pair.** *(1) It is not stencil-clipped.* The pick pipeline descriptor carries depth format/write/compare and **no stencil state at all** (`WebGPUVector3DTilePrimitiveRenderer.js:535-550`), where WebGL emits a stencil-tested pick state (`Scene/Vector3DTilePrimitive.js:713-736` `pickRenderState`: `stencilTest.enabled: true` `:715`, `NOT_EQUAL` `:716`/`:722`, `reference: 0` `:728`, `mask`/`stencilMask` `CLASSIFICATION_MASK` `:729`/`:731`). `scene.pick` therefore returns the right feature id for the **wrong footprint** — a hit anywhere in the inflated volume silhouette rather than the stencil-clipped surface∩volume region. **Narrowed:** drop the "sky-side" clause — `pickFS:286-288` already discards sky. *(2) The depth states are opposite, and only the WebGPU pick samples depth.* WebGL's `pickRenderState` disables the depth test outright (`:732-733 depthTest: { enabled: false }`, with `depthMask: false` `:735`), while the WebGPU `pickDescriptor` sets `depthCompare: "less-equal"` (`:548`) **and** binds a depth-sample bind group the classify path does not carry (`layout` `:537` -> `:407-410` `bindGroupLayouts: [sharedBgl, depthSampleBgl]`, against `classifyLayout` `:418-421` `[sharedBgl]`; the pick pipeline's execution is gated on that bind group at `:1623`). **A fixer must not assume the two states differ only in stencil** — moving pick onto the stencil path also has to decide what happens to the depth test and the depth sample, and the same file's colour pipeline (`:502-532`, `classifyLayout` `:504`, `depthCompare: "always"` `:515`, full stencil block `:516-529`) is the shape to port from, not the current pick descriptor. The limitation is documented in the renderer's own docstring at `:40-42`, and no ledger row owns the pick path (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:9988` is the shipped colour path; `:9996` is the 2D/CV row) — **whoever fixes it updates that docstring in the same commit**, or the batch's dominant pattern (a rationale in prose that the code has outgrown) recurs immediately. | DEFECT | as cited | **unowned** |

`WGH-9`, `WGH-10` and `WGH-11` share one consequence, which the verifier established **once** on the binning code rather than three times on assertion: `Scene/View.js:320` gates on `defined(boundingVolume)`; a command without one takes the worst-case `[frustum.near, frustum.far]` at `:377-378` (inside the `else` of the no-bounding-volume branch, comment `:375-376`) and is inserted into **every overlapping frustum slice** by `insertIntoBin` (`:627-649`), whose `break` at `:646-648` is gated on `executeInClosestFrustum`, which nothing here sets; `ViewportExecutor.js:436`/`:845` runs the potentially-visible set on both backends and the WebGPU renderer consumes the same `frustumCommandsList` and executes per slice. **The consequence survives intact: the classification command is drawn once per frustum slice instead of once.**

### 3.7 Shadows & lighting

| id | Finding (corrected wording) | Verdict | Current `file:line` | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `B.11` | The WebGPU CSM cast list omits **volumetric casters** (Voxel, GaussianSplat). Cascaded shadow maps take casters only from renderers that set `_shadowCastLayout` — models, globe tiles and geometry primitives. The voxel and splat renderers set it nowhere; both only _receive_. Upstream WebGL does not cast from them either, and screen-space collections are deliberate non-goals on both backends. Casting from volumetric content needs a deep-shadow-map or transmittance-accumulation technique rather than a cast-layout registration, so it stays demand-gated. **Scope corrected:** the id is _not_ "defined nowhere" — it has a disposition row and a demand gate. The action is narrower than first judged: **mirror the existing disposition into `DEFERRED_WORK.md`** (grep: zero hits) so the demand gate lives in the ledger the campaigns read, and carry the mechanism sentence above. | CORRECTED | `WebGPUCSMCastPass.ts:324`, `:328`; `WebGPUCSMRenderer.ts:237`; `WebGPUGlobeSurfaceTileBuffers.ts:541`, `:557`; `WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:875`, `:7726`; `WebGPUPrimitiveCommands.ts:407`; `WebGPUPrimitiveShadowCast.ts:28`, `:248`, `:264`; `WebGPUShadowMapRenderer.js:668`; `Scene/GlobeSurfaceTileProviderRendering.js:1506`; receive-only uniforms at `WebGPUVoxelRenderer.ts:375-378`, `WebGPUGaussianSplatRenderer.ts:592-595` | `ISSUES_AND_FIXED_BUGS.md:188` (DEFER / OPEN by design) + `NEXT_QUEUE_2026-07-04.md:101` (demand gate); **absent from `DEFERRED_WORK.md`** |
| `H-R2` / `H-P20` | Shadow cast uses a dedicated cast pipeline family, but the cast fragment shader is empty — **scope narrowed.** WebGL does **not** keep an alpha test for MASK materials (`ShadowMap.js:257` sets `isOpaque = command.pass !== Pass.TRANSLUCENT`, `AlphaPipelineStage.js:22` puts MASK in `opaquePass`, `ShadowMapShader.js:134-137` omits `czm_shadow_cast_main()` for the opaque-and-unclipped case), so a masked leaf casts its full quad on **both** backends and that half is at parity. The two real residuals are **(a) TRANSLUCENT-pass casters**, where WebGL runs the colour main and discards at `out_FragColor.a == 0.0` and WebGPU's empty `shadowCastFragment` cannot; and **(b) clipping-plane discard on the cast**, which WebGL preserves via `hasClipping` while the WebGPU cast shader has no clip term, so clipped geometry still casts. Fix shape: a cast variant that samples baseColor and evaluates clipping planes, plus a probe over a translucent-textured and a clipped caster. | CORRECTED | pipelines `WebGPUShadowMapRenderer.js:160`, `:666`, `:819-823`, depth-only `:104`, `:113`; residual `Shaders/WebGPU/Shadow/ShadowMap.wgsl:47` (`shadowCastFragment()` takes no inputs, empty body; 0 `alpha`/`discard`/`clip` hits); WebGL `Scene/ShadowMap.js:257`, `:264-266`, `Scene/ShadowMapShader.js:134-147`, `Scene/Model/AlphaPipelineStage.js:22` | **unowned** for the residuals; `QUEUE_2026-07-18_CAMPAIGN11.md:2165` `C11-189` (NOT STARTED) covers the style half only |
| `S6.2-C11-184` | `C11-184` stays IN PROGRESS until the browser gates execute: the W1 row reads "FOCUSED + MOVING RUNTIME GATES OPEN", the globe-RECEIVE leg was discharged at Batch 850 and everything else is explicitly left open. `Scene.js` keeps `cascadesEnabled` at its default `true` on both backends. | CONFIRMED | `QUEUE_2026-07-18_CAMPAIGN11.md:2126`, `:2533`; `Scene/Scene.js:1138-1148` | `C11-184` |
| `#59` | WebGPU CSM ignores `ShadowMap.numberOfCascades`. `_cascadeCount` is configurable but `numberOfCascades` has **zero** hits under `Renderer/` — no site threads `ShadowMap._numberOfCascades` (validated to 1 or 4) into that config, so a WebGL-valid `numberOfCascades: 1` shadow map still runs four cascades on WebGPU. Verify with a probe and wire the count. LOW-MEDIUM. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUCSMRenderer.ts:405`; source value `Scene/ShadowMap.js:502-504`, validation `:376-378` | **unowned** |
| `#56` / `A7.3` | The CSM cast-cull SoA `centerX/Y/Z/radius` fields are filled per cast command per cascade per frame while the file's own comment states only `interleaved` is uploaded. Either use the SoA as a debug surface or drop it. LOW, deliberate per comment. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUCSMCastPass.ts:534-539` (alloc), `:562-570` (fill), comment `:543-545` | **unowned** |
| `#58` / `A7.5` | Per-cast-pass `Cartesian3` allocations: `enc.high ?? new Cartesian3()`, `enc.low ?? new Cartesian3()` and a third `new Cartesian3(...)` run per cast invocation. Hold a module scratch and set `x/y/z`. LOW. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUCSMCastPass.ts:467-470` | **unowned** |
| `#62` | The cast pass has no drawn-vs-skipped sentinel: it `continue`s silently on every unresolved command shape and never counts drawn versus skipped per cascade. A cascade that draws 0 of N>0 commands should emit a throttled permanent warning. LOW, diagnostic. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUCSMCastPass.ts` — 0 matches for `skipped` / `drawnCount` | **unowned** |

### 3.8 Post-process, effects & attachment topology

| id | Finding (corrected wording) | Verdict | Current `file:line` | Owner |

Lazy allocation on the first active-stage frame remains the unowned fix. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUPostProcessPipeline.ts:611-627`, `:1510-1513` | **unowned**; adjacent `RENDERER_LANDSCAPE_AUDIT_2026-09-02.md:233` (THREE-A09) |

The original finding stands **in full**, not partially. | `#73` / `A8.7` / `F5-10` / `ACTION-13` | The OIT injected-pipeline cache is keyed on `` `${shaderCode.length}_${fragEntry}_${config.label ?? ""}` `` — a shader-**length** key, so two different shaders of equal length sharing an entry point and label collide, and the same key serves both the pipeline and the shader-module cache. Separately, the composite bind group is rebuilt unconditionally on every update. #### 3.8 — 2026-09-04 continuation (lens re-run, batch L2)

Source: `lighting.bank.md` (Tar-Atanamir / Tar-Telemmaite, **COMPLETE — qualified**), adjudicated by `REVIEW_L2_IVORWEN.md`, whose one BLOCK verdict in the batch landed here and **tiered `L2-LGT-4` down from "broken at defaults" to "broken in the opted-in FAR-003 containment configuration"**. Verifier wording; the reachability sentence below is Ivorwen's replacement text verbatim.

| `L2-LGT-4` | **Highest-stakes claim in the batch.** The OIT accumulation render pass attaches the scene framebuffer's **multisampled** depth-stencil view to two **single-sampled** colour attachments (`WebGPUOIT.ts:206-222` creates both targets with no `sampleCount`; `getAccumulationPassDescriptor` `:156-160` binds `{ view, depthReadOnly, stencilReadOnly }` with **no resolve target**; source `WebGPUSceneRendererTranslucentPass.ts:193-195` → `WebGPURenderTarget.ts:487-489` → the attachment created at `:220-227` with `sampleCount: sampleCount!`), so **under MSAA the pass cannot be encoded at all**. **Reachability, checked and tiered down from the lead's claim:** the depth attachment has **no resolve target**, so the bound view is necessarily the MSAA one; `shouldAllocateWebGPUOIT` (`WebGPUSceneRendererEnsureResources.ts:163-168`) has **no MSAA term**; and `msaaSamples` defaults to 4 (`Scene.js:576`). But the fourth condition does **not** hold: the native-OIT safety gate `host._webgpuOITEnabled` is declared `false` (`WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:952`) and no in-tree code enables it at startup — the only writer is the public setter at `:1250`, reached from `CesiumDebug.webgpuOIT(true)` (`Scene/CesiumDebug.js:813`). At defaults `host._oit` is never constructed (`WebGPUSceneRendererEnsureResources.ts:372-373`) and the accumulation pass is never encoded (`WebGPUSceneRendererTranslucentPass.ts:133`). **This is therefore broken in the opted-in FAR-003 containment configuration, not at defaults** — which is the tier at which it should be prioritised. The containment posture is stated in the engine at `WebGPUOIT.ts:164-168` and at the gate's declaration (`WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:948-952`, *"Default false preserves the complete alpha-blend fallback"*), and pinned by `Specs/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUUnsafeDefaultsSpec.js:596-600`, which asserts `shouldAllocateWebGPUOIT(true, false) === false`. **Counter-citation trap, named so it is not walked into twice:** `Specs/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRendererDependentResourcesSpec.js:114` reads `_webgpuOITEnabled: options.oitEnabled ?? true` — a **test fixture forcing the gate on**, not a production default. | DEFECT | as cited | **amends** `B7` (this document, `:254`) and `NEW-WEBGPU-OIT-MSAA-RESOLVE-ORDERING` (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:10377`) — **scoped to the contained path.** The default-path version of the amendment is **withdrawn**: their compositing-order framing presumes an encode that does not happen, but only once `CesiumDebug.webgpuOIT(true)` has been set |

Restated: *the depth gate returns `uv` wherever per-frame camera motion along that pixel's view ray exceeds `max(0.1% of that pixel's eye depth, 1 m)` — routine for near-ground dollies and forward `flyTo`s, not universal for lateral orbits or high-altitude views.* | DEFECT | as cited | **amends** `#81` (this document, `:261`), whose LOW / threshold-tuning premise treats a constant as the whole defect |

**This section is the repoint target for `ARCHITECTURE.md:969-974`**, the only live pointer that sent a reader into a retired file _for content_. The owning row is PARTIAL and says so in terms ("nested/device-loss walk open"), with a single landing commit. | CONFIRMED | `WebGPUDeviceLossRecovery.ts:27-31`, `:217`, `:240`; `WebGPUSceneRendererEnsureResources.ts:233`, `:583-604`, `:652-653`; `WebGPULabelRenderer.js:819-820`, `:1348`; `Scene/PointPrimitiveCollection.js:886-893`, `Scene/LabelCollection.js:1052-1064`; `QUEUE_2026-07-18_CAMPAIGN11.md:1639`, `:2452`, narrative `:1311-1313` | `C11-20` / `C-R12-PER-OBJECT-CACHES` (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:8360`) / FAR-208 |

### 3.12 Precision, RTE & ellipsoid assumptions

| id | Finding (corrected wording) | Verdict | Current `file:line` | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `H19` / `#75` / `A9.2` | Previous-frame model motion vectors abandon the RTE path: `prevPositionMC` is reassembled by adding `translationHigh + translationLow`, then transformed by `previousModelMatrix` and `camera.previousViewProjection` in **single-precision world space**, while the current frame uses `mvpRelativeToEye`. The shader comment accepts "the residual metre of precision loss". Effect: transient multi-pixel velocity noise during fast fly-to, matching the voxel TAA pattern. A `previousViewProjectionRelativeToEye` + `previousModelMatrixRelativeToEye` pair would close it. MEDIUM, accepted. | CONFIRMED | `Shaders/WebGPU/Model/ModelPBRComplete.wgsl:1021-1023`, `:1030-1031`, `:1039-1042` | `C11-53` / FAR-306 for the finding; the RTE-pair fix is **unowned** |

### 3.15 Method & governance — the eleven principles (`F5-PRINCIPLES`, `GATE-4-ACCEPTANCE`)

`FABLE5_PROGRESS_AND_ACTION_AUDIT_2026-07-22.md` carries a method register that no successor fully states. Its eleven principles already exist as one-line statements in `CODEX_DISAGREEMENTS_WITH_FABLE_REVIEW_2026-08-16.md:60-71`, which is why the file is archivable rather than KEEP-PART — **but that packet's 33 links are line anchors into the archived file** and resolve only against git history after the move. Reproduce them under stable headings there or here.

**Three clauses exist nowhere else and must be restored explicitly, or they die with the file:**

1. **Bounded visual evidence.** A route's cross-backend diff validates the subject it rendered (offline ellipsoid + NaturalEarthII) — never real terrain, water, dense 3D Tiles, OIT, or every post-process consumer.
2. **Honest incomplete test state.** A stopped suite is neither a complete green nor a complete red, and unrelated lint debt stays separately attributed.
3. **`REJECT-4` — the control-leg clause.** `CLAUDE.md:20` and SR-1 (`QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:40`) carry only the **prohibition** half: never remove, default-disable, bypass or visually degrade a feature to win a metric. The distinctive half is stated nowhere else in the tracked tree: **a feature toggle is a legitimate A/B attribution control, and the OFF leg is never the shipped fix.** SR-9 covers interleaved A/B for GPU timing only. The first reader dropped this clause from `GATE-4-ACCEPTANCE`'s text as well as retiring the item, so it must be restored in **both** places.

**`GATE-4-ACCEPTANCE` (corrected).** With the control-leg clause restored: disabling OIT, clustered lighting, the scheduler, the octree, shadows or post-processing is **not an optimisation** — an OFF leg is an attribution control. Then, as first judged: compare against the current `Tools/visual-regression` baselines; add temporal sequences for TAA history, RTE under motion and altitude transitions; and any pass-folding or clear-reordering change additionally needs numeric `pickPosition`, primitive/billboard pick, classification, depth-plane/horizon, multi-frustum, off-camera shadow-caster, HDR, 2D/Columbus, resize and device-recovery checks **with the feature on**.

| `M-P7` | REFUTED | Material state **is** in the primitive-cache invalidation comparison: `WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:6560-6563` includes `hasMaterialGenerationChanged(...)` (defined `:3392-3401`, comparing `_materialBase` identity with `_effectiveAlphaMode`) in the predicate that destroys and deletes the entry. Runtime factor and colour edits need no invalidation at all — they reach the GPU through `packMaterialUniforms` every frame (`:2111`, called `:6874`, `:8333`). CustomShader swaps rebuild on reference change and cascade translucency. The only residue is an in-place mutation of a **retained** `MaterialInfo` identity that changes a texture reader without changing `alphaMode`, a shape no loader in the tree produces. The distinct customShader-invalidation defect (`A5-3`) is a separate row and stays where it is. |
| `GUARDS` | REFUTED | The behavioural degenerate-triangle spec the audit demanded **exists**: `Specs/Scene/Model/EdgeVisibilityPipelineStageDecodingSpec.js:662` builds a primitive with two coincident vertices, documented at `:737-742` as the upstream #13421 repro, with both artefacts recorded shipped at Batch 299 (`DEFERRED_WORK.md:4933`). The source-text regex guard is now redundant defence-in-depth beside a real spec, not the sole certification. The `git diff --check` and material-probe halves are already closed. **No new tools row is needed.** |
| `H-P11` | CORRECTED to closed — do not re-file | The WebGL-parity premise is false. `Scene/ClippingPolygonCollection.js:300-307` performs the **identical** count-only check, with an upstream comment giving the reason. The WebGPU path is if anything stricter, gating on `lastTotalPositions`, `collection.length` **and** a non-null texture. The curvature half is likewise shared: both SDFs are planar in lat/lon space with no curvature term. This is **not a WebGPU parity gap**; if it survives at all it is an upstream-shared enhancement note whose home is an upstream issue, not a fork queue row. |
| `S9-4` ADR-3 half | CORRECTED to closed — do not re-open | The first reader asked whether the model camera arena is a live ADR-3 ownership violation. It is not: the arena is **context-owned**, stated in both the context getter's comment and the arena's own header, and the device pool holds no arena at all (Batch 819 `ad43f8c527`). Carry only the certification remainder and the stale `C11-195` clause (§3.9). |

**Latent note, carried from `H-P9` and `H-P11`, not a finding.** Two caches in the tree are keyed on a **proxy for identity rather than on identity itself** and are currently safe only because nothing mutates in place: `WebGPUPointCloudRenderer.ts:1789` sets `const revision = pointCount`, sound only because each `TimeDynamicPointCloud` frame is a fresh instance (`Scene/TimeDynamicPointCloud.js:540`, `Scene/PointCloud.js:194`, `:695-710`) and tileset PNTS content is parsed once; and the clipping-polygon SDF above. Worth one sentence in any future caching lens.

#### 3.16 — 2026-09-04 continuation (lens re-run, batch L2)

| id | Why it does not hold at HEAD |
| --- | --- |
| `L2-LGT-7` | *"`WebGPUOIT.update()` recreates every target on a device change but keeps its two pipeline/shader-module caches, so a device-loss recovery serves pipelines belonging to the destroyed device."* **REFUTED.** The caches do not survive the device change because the whole `WebGPUOIT` object does not: `WebGPUSceneRendererEnsureResources.ts:193-198` drops `host._oit = null` on device invalidation (intent stated at `:190-192`; note `host._oit = null` is at `:198`, one line past the lead's cited range), `:372-373` constructs a fresh `WebGPUOIT` whose two caches are **field initialisers** and therefore empty (`WebGPUOIT.ts:384-385`) on the next ensure pass, and the invalidation genuinely fires on the recovery path (`WebGPUDeviceLossRecovery.ts:556` → `WebGPUContext._clearAllCaches` `:7700` → `_fireDeviceInvalidated()` `:7830`). `update()`'s `this._device !== device` branch (`:189-193`) is **defensive scaffolding for a path that does not occur** — which is also why nobody clearing the caches there was never noticed as a bug. Every citation in the finding is exact; the conclusion drawn from them is not. **What would settle it the other way:** a second device handed to a surviving SceneRenderer outside the loss-recovery flow, i.e. without `_clearAllCaches`. Two independent readers searched for one and neither could construct it at HEAD. *Residual not re-filed:* the finding's secondary sentence about commands retaining `cmd._oitPipeline` is a different mechanism (per-renderer cache invalidation) and is not settled by the above. |

**One item, one refutation.** Batch L2 returned **zero UNADJUDICATED** across 42 findings; this is the only one that did not survive.

The 2026-09-02 plan's eighteen lenses named none of: WebGL health / dual-backend divergence, scene modes (2D / CV / morph), workers & concurrency, widgets & public API, untrusted input, or upstream architectural drift. WebGL-health and scene-mode findings (`WGH-*`) and public-API findings (`arch-api-*`) are filed under their subsystem headings above (`WGH-1`, `WGH-5` §3.1; `WGH-7` §3.4; `WGH-8` §3.6; `WGH-2` §3.9; `WGH-6`, `arch-api-4/6/7` §3.10; `arch-api-9` §3.3; `arch-api-1..5` §3.13; `WGH-3/4`, `arch-api-8` §3.14). #### 3.17.3 Workers & concurrency

Source: `shaderasync.bank.md` (Damrod / Mablung, COMPLETE). §3 above carried zero `TaskProcessor` / `Source/Workers` rows. `1390-10 [shaderasync]` (no worker error handler) is filed under §3.11.

| id | Statement (verifier wording) | Kind | Current `file:line` | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `1390-9 [shaderasync]` | "Concurrency" in the worker layer is a queue-depth throttle on one thread: `TaskProcessor` owns exactly one `Worker`, created lazily (`:221-231`); `maximumActiveTasks` caps in-flight tasks against that worker and returns `undefined` past the cap (`:226-228`); `runTask` posts to `processor._worker` (`:347-375`). **Five** subsystems compute `hardwareConcurrency - 1` and pass it there under names that say otherwise (`DracoLoader:91`, `GaussianSplatSorter:14`, `GaussianSplatTextureGenerator:9`, `I3SDecoder:20`, `MVTTileDecoder:27`); terrain uses three processors, each internally serial (`QuantizedMeshTerrainData.js:583-593`; cap 5 at `TerrainData.js:124`); the only real pool is `PrimitiveGeometryHelpers.js:274-327`; 27 module-level `new TaskProcessor(` sites. Inherited from upstream, but the fork added worker types on the pattern. | GAP | as cited | **unowned, unsurveyed** |

**Did not survive / not reached (3.17.3).** The shaderasync bank did not read the 45 `TaskProcessor` consumers individually, `RendererWorker.js` internals, or compare any WGSL/GLSL pair for drift. `WorkerSceneHost` / `RendererWorker` reachability was verifier-confirmed as a real off-main-thread path (healthy).

## 4. Reversals

Five items a first reader had marked **resolved, superseded or credited-as-fixed** that the second readers found **still live**. They come from four documents. Each is a reason a record is wrong today, independent of what happens to the archived text, and each gets a proposed row.

### R1 — Hi-Z occlusion is DEFAULT OFF in code while the ledger says DEFAULT ON

Judged RESOLVED on the strength of `migration_doc/DEFERRED_WORK.md:5338`, which reads:

> `### FORK-41 — Hi-Z occlusion: RESOLVED (C2-21, 2026-06-24) — root cause was a depth-source bug; command-drop now DEFAULT ON, verified`

The code says otherwise:

- `packages/engine/Source/Renderer/WebGPU/WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:1189` — `private _hiZConsumeEnabled: boolean = false;`
- `:1191-1196` — "It remains disabled until result identity is tied to the producing frame, frustum, and command list."
- `:4195-4198` — "Do not drop commands by default until each result identifies its producing frustum, frame, and command generation." followed by `if (!this._hiZConsumeEnabled) return commands;`

So the 2026-04-30 finding is **still true in its operative sense**: no command is dropped by Hi-Z on the default path. Two consequences point in different directions.

1. **The finding itself is tracked**, so it does not by itself keep a document alive: `QUEUE_2026-07-18_CAMPAIGN11.md:1770` (`C11-98`, FORK-41, W7) owns it, and `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:727-729` states the ownership explicitly.
2. **A live ledger row asserts a state HEAD contradicts.** `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:605` independently verified default-off on 2026-08-29 ("Three containment switches, all default-off, all verified 2026-08-29: … FORK-41 Hi-Z command drop `WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:1181` `_hiZConsumeEnabled = false`"). The `:5338` heading is the stale one; the superseded `:5365` PARTIAL entry below it describes the actual state better. This is exactly the kind of row a future brief would cite as a premise — the failure CLAUDE.md Principle 10 was written against.

Two verifiers reached this row independently (also flagged under `B4`, §3.9).

> **Proposed row — `NEW-FORK41-LEDGER-ROW-CORRECTION` (S, docs).** Correct `DEFERRED_WORK.md:5338` to state Hi-Z command-drop is **default OFF** with the `_hiZConsumeEnabled = false` citation and the reason, demote the "RESOLVED … DEFAULT ON, verified" heading, and leave `C11-98` as the owner of the wiring. Acceptance: the row and the code agree, and `B4`'s owning record can be quoted without contradiction.

### R2 — `debugShowBoundingVolume` is still dropped for four collections

`M-R5` was judged RESOLVED with the text "Definition only: resolved to WebGL parity". That holds for the `Buffer*` / `GeoJsonPrimitive` family only. For the four collections `M-R5`'s own title names, the flag is dropped:

- **WebGL sets it on the command** — `Scene/BillboardCollection.js:1220`, `Scene/PointPrimitiveCollection.js:842`, `Scene/PolylineCollection.js:802` and `:898`.
- **The WebGPU renderers never do** — `grep -c debugShowBoundingVolume` returns **0** for `WebGPUBillboardRenderer.js`, `WebGPULabelRenderer.js`, `WebGPUPointPrimitiveRenderer.js` and `WebGPUPolylineRenderer.js`. Their commands carry `owner` and `boundingVolume` (e.g. `WebGPUBillboardRenderer.js:1355-1356`), so culling works, but not the debug flag.
- **The consumer filters on exactly that flag** — `WebGPUSceneRenderer.ts:3652` skips any command with `cmd.debugShowBoundingVolume !== true`.
- **The WebGL assignment is unreachable on WebGPU** — `BillboardCollection.js:750` takes the feature-renderer early return well before the command-build block at `:1195-1228`.
- **No row owns it** — `DEFERRED_WORK.md:5012` is scoped to the three `Buffer*` renderers and `GeoJsonPrimitive`.

Net effect: setting `billboardCollection.debugShowBoundingVolume = true` (or the label, point or polyline equivalent) draws the red wireframe on WebGL and nothing on WebGPU. Severity is debug-tooling parity, not rendering correctness — but the item migrates as **PARTIAL**, not resolved.

> **Proposed row — `NEW-WEBGPU-COLLECTION-BV-DEBUG-DRAW` (S).** Set `command.debugShowBoundingVolume` from the collection in the four WebGPU collection renderers, as the `Buffer*` lane already does. Acceptance: a probe that sets the flag on a billboard, label, point and polyline collection and captures a non-empty wireframe on both backends.

### R3 — the globe material packing fix was credited but did not land

`H17` was kept open, but credited with "packing now happens once per frame per material type rather than per tile". **False.** `_getOrCreateMaterialPipeline` (`WebGPUGlobeSurfaceRenderer.ts:522`) is invoked at `:1625` inside `createTileCommands` (`:1099`), which `Scene/GlobeSurfaceTileProviderRendering.js:1356` calls **once per tile**; `:650-654` runs `packMaterialUBO` — a fresh `ArrayBuffer` and `Float32Array` at `WebGPUGlobeMaterial.ts:396-397` — and `queue.writeBuffer` on **every** invocation. Only the pipeline and shader-module cache entry (`:543-588`) is per `material.type`. The original headline — thousands of redundant allocations and queue writes per frame on a material-enabled globe — is **unmitigated**. It is inert while `globe.material` is unset, which is the default.

> **Proposed row — `NEW-GLOBE-MATERIAL-UBO-PER-FRAME` (M, under FAR-303).** Hoist the material pack and upload to once per frame per `material.type`, keyed on the material's own generation, and reuse a retained `ArrayBuffer`. Acceptance: a `globe.material`-enabled scene shows `writeBuffer` calls for the material UBO proportional to material types, not to visible tiles, on the moving-altitude campaign; byte-identical capture with the material set.

### R4 — `RenderCommand` has zero real importers while CLAUDE.md tells new features to use it

`H9`'s "RenderCommand now has production consumers" is **false**, and the parallel `D1` finding rests on the identical grep defect.

`Renderer/WebGPU/RenderCommand.js` has **no importer anywhere in `packages/engine/Source`**. Seven files mention the string; **zero** contain `from ".../RenderCommand.js"`. The only *import* of it outside the module itself is the generated barrel `packages/engine/index.js:1025`, and the cited "consumers" are local functions coincidentally named `updateAndQueueRenderCommand` (`Scene/ClassificationPrimitive.js:1300`, `Scene/GroundPrimitive.js:802`; `Scene/QuadtreePrimitive.js:1365` is the same shape). The remaining mentions are doc comments (`Renderer/GraphicsContext.ts:1903-1904`, `Stubs/WebGLStubShader.ts:16`, `:392`, `WebGLCompatibilityStub.ts:102`, `:128`, `:134`). 25 Scene files construct `DrawCommand` directly, and `FEATURE_INVENTORY.md:512` already tags the module SCAFFOLDED / UNUSED.

Meanwhile **CLAUDE.md's architecture section instructs that new scene features SHOULD use `RenderCommand` instead of importing backend-specific commands directly.** The backend-agnostic command abstraction the fork tells its own contributors to prefer is dead on arrival. Corrected, the finding is *stronger* than recorded; migrating either text verbatim would carry the false "3 Scene files adopt RenderCommand" figure forward.

Two second-order consequences: the webgl-only throwing-stub path for this module is unreachable, so the fleet1 exemption row is a latent-on-adoption hazard rather than a live defect (§3.10 `D3`); and `RenderCommand` is **not** on the compat-exemption list, contrary to the original claim.

> **Proposed row — `NEW-RENDERCOMMAND-ADOPT-OR-RETIRE` (M, architecture decision).** Rule the abstraction in or out. If in: one concrete, measured consumer first (the pattern `REJECT-6` demands for `ResourceOwnership`), plus the CLAUDE.md guidance kept. If out: retire the module, drop the barrel export, and **replace the CLAUDE.md instruction in the same commit** so the guidance never points at a retired module. Acceptance either way: no rule in `CLAUDE.md` names a zero-importer module.

### R5 — `REJECT-4`'s control-leg clause survives nowhere

`REJECT-4` is marked SUPERSEDED against `CLAUDE.md:20` / SR-1, but those carry only the **prohibition** half — "never remove, default-disable, bypass or visually degrade a feature to win a metric". The distinctive half, **_a feature toggle is a legitimate A/B attribution control, and the OFF leg is never the shipped fix_**, is stated nowhere else in the tracked tree; the whole SR-1..SR-17 table was read (`QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:38-55`) and SR-9 covers interleaved A/B for GPU timing only. The first reader additionally dropped the same clause from `GATE-4-ACCEPTANCE`'s migrate text, whose own title still contains it. On the judgement as written, the rule dies with the file.

> **Proposed row — `NEW-SR-CONTROL-LEG-CLAUSE` (S, governance).** Add the clause to the SR table beside SR-1 and restore it in `GATE-4-ACCEPTANCE` (§3.15). Acceptance: the clause is stated in a live governance document, not only in an archived audit.

**Adjacent corrections that are not reversals but travel with them** — the four stale-green `FIX_QUEUE` rows, the `MAINTAINABILITY` `T10` / `N8` row that turned out to **have** an owner recording a reproduced defect, `ULTRA` `#35` which `DEFERRED_WORK.md:9842` already owns, `PER_FEATURE` `H-P11` / `M-P7` / `H-P2` held open against repaired code, `LOCAL_CHANGE`'s over-cautious ADR-3 carry, and the refuted `GUARDS` item — are recorded in §3.14 and §3.16.

## 6. Pending sections — honestly marked

Two 2026-09-02 documents belong to this review and are **not** folded into §3. Their status differs sharply.

### 6.1 The phase-1 internal register — PARTIAL AND UNRELIABLE, do not cite

[`audits/2026-09-02_ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_PHASE1.md`](audits/2026-09-02_ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_PHASE1.md) is linked here for provenance only. **Its findings are not reproduced in this file, and must not be quoted as premises.** Two independent reasons:

1. **Two of eighteen lenses ran.** The document discloses this itself: the verified inputs come from `gltf-models` and `geometry-primitives` only. The other sixteen — globe & imagery, 3D Tiles, collections, entity/datasource, picking, shadows/lighting, post-process, performance & compute, architecture/build, ownership-lifetime, frame-graph, shader-composition, precision-parity, async-and-readiness, testability-observability, planned-work-fit — produced **no verified output**, so its §4 cross-cutting synthesis is derived from what two lenses saw. Five further candidates (`GLTF-MODELS-1..5`) carry **no recorded vote** and fail its own survival bar without having been refuted.
2. **Four of its citations are verified false at HEAD.** Read this session:
   - `GEOMETRY-PRIMITIVES-1` ("one colour uniform, hard-coded red under `PerInstanceColorAppearance`") cites `WebGPUGroundPrimitiveRenderer.js:2132`. At HEAD `:2132` is a comment about reusing the inner primitive's pick ID, and `:1798-1801` reads `packClassificationColor(data, 24, primitive)` with a comment stating that a `PerInstanceColorAppearance` keeps its colour on the geometry instance "the way WebGL's `PER_INSTANCE_COLOR` varying does". **Per-instance classification colour landed at Batch 1389 (`e337646ea4`)**, so the finding's headline no longer holds.
   - `GEOMETRY-PRIMITIVES-2` ("bakes only `color`/`depthFailColor` at command build") cites `WebGPUPrimitiveCommands.ts:3002-3014`. At HEAD that block reads `primitive._batchTable`, `_batchTableAttributeIndices.color` and `.depthFailColor` and derives `hasInstanceColors` / `hasDepthFail`, so the anchor no longer supports the quoted claim. Whether the `show` / `offset` / `distanceDisplayCondition` half survives is **undetermined** and needs its own re-derivation.
   - **§2 and Appendix A** praise "a failed pipeline swaps to an explicit magenta `ErrorPipeline`" and cite `WebGPUModelRenderer.ts:3513-3557`. There are **zero** `ErrorPipeline` hits in that range — the symbol lives in `WebGPUModelPipelineCache.ts` (9 hits), and `:3513` is `function getCustomShaderEntries(`.
   - **§2** praises "the shadow-cast path reproduces the skinned VS rather than casting from the rest pose" and cites `WebGPUPrimitiveShadowCast.ts:277-283`. That 290-line file contains **no** `skin` reference.

   The last two are claims that a path is **correct**, so their falsity means the behaviour is unverified rather than broken — but the register errs in both directions, which is why nothing in it may be briefed without re-derivation. The document now carries this list as a banner at its head.

**Disposition (2026-09-03).** The phase-1 register is superseded by the 2026-09-03 lens re-run, whose verified findings are the "2026-09-03 survey" blocks under each §3 heading and §3.17; the plan it ran under ([`ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_PLAN_2026-09-02.md`](ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_PLAN_2026-09-02.md)) remains the lens specification. The register stays tracked for provenance only — nothing in it may be briefed, filed or cited without re-derivation at HEAD.

### 6.2 The external landscape audit — usable; its ranked HIGH gaps

[`RENDERER_LANDSCAPE_AUDIT_2026-09-02.md`](RENDERER_LANDSCAPE_AUDIT_2026-09-02.md) is usable as written: it is an external-comparison audit (three.js `WebGPURenderer` + TSL, luma.gl 9 / deck.gl 9, the WebGPU platform, CesiumJS upstream and the geospatial field, plus PlayCanvas, Babylon.js and Bevy / wgpu / naga since its 2026-09-03 revision) with a per-claim census appendix. Its per-claim census was re-derived on 2026-09-03 (lane Erchirion) from the verification re-run: all 177 collected claims carry verifier votes (171 kept, 2 refuted, 4 split, 0 unverified), and the three added engines were mapped against the fork at `file:line` beside the original four. The HIGH list below did not change under that pass. Its rows remain ranked leads with named demonstrating targets — external claims verified at their sources, fork state read at `file:line` — not adjudicated findings of this review.

Its ranked **HIGH** gaps are reproduced here so the ranking is visible from the review authority. Ordering is by (unblocks-others, correctness risk, effort). Its MEDIUM list, "what NOT to copy" section and claim census are **not** reproduced; read them there.

| Gap | Statement | Demonstrated by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **G1** | Take the 1.145 sync and re-derive the WGSL twin it invalidates. Unlocks C12, C13, C14, C09. **Corrected 2026-09-04 against [`UPSTREAM_SYNC_PLAN_1.145_2026-09-04.md`](UPSTREAM_SYNC_PLAN_1.145_2026-09-04.md) §7.3** (lane U; refutation CONFIRMED by its station-3 reviewer), on two counts. (1) **The S3 clause is REFUTED and is not a gate on this sync.** `C12-29` S3 is `NEW-ECLIPSE-OCCLUSION-EFFECTS` (`QUEUE_2026-07-19_CAMPAIGN12.md:36`) — eclipse occlusion and shadow contrast — and `R-2026-09-02-5` funds its exposure-sweep discriminator. Neither is a classification depth target and neither bears on polygon draping; running it would consume an Edge leg for a green tick that certifies nothing about the merge. Two of lane U's workers independently failed to confirm the linkage and a third refuted it. S3 remains live work under its own ruling — just not this sync's business. (2) **The "clipping-polygon holes in the SDF atlas" remedy is stale**: 1.145 added holes to the **vector** path and **deleted** the SDF one, so the prescribed remedy targets an algorithm that no longer exists upstream. The row is one twin, not two — `RL-01a` re-scopes from "SDF-atlas holes" to "the WGSL twin of `vectorClip`", at which point it merges with `RL-01b` (both need the same polygon tables) and both are blocked on the already-open `NEW-WEBGPU-VECTOR-POLYGON-DRAPING`. | CesiumJS 1.145 |
| **G2** | Guard the RTE error-free transforms against **Metal reassociation** and add an Apple-adapter lane. The fork's hard 64-bit rule is silently voided on one vendor's compiler: the RTE chunk computes `highDifference + lowDifference` as plain f32 with **zero** contraction guards, and no Apple/Safari/macOS lane exists in `Tools/`. Risk if ignored: the FAR-camera L0-tile jitter class fixed at Batch 350 reappears for Safari and Chrome-on-macOS users with no probe able to detect it. | luma.gl 9.4 (`LUMA-G3`) |
| **G3** | Populate `ShaderDefine` bits from `navigator.gpu.wgslLanguageFeatures` and route `enable` emission through the preprocessor. Today there is **one** device-derived bit, **0** hits for `wgslLanguageFeatures`, and `enable` directives are string-prepended per renderer or duplicated into 13 `*_f16.wgsl` twins — prepends under the same define mask are exactly the aliasing class Batch 825 closed at the pipeline level. Enabler for the later platform rows. | WGSL CRD; three.js per-shader `enable` |
| **G4** | Wire **dual-source blending** as the single-pass translucent composite, sidestepping the multi-frustum MSAA topology FAR-404 plans for the MRT path. The feature is requested and exposed but the variant is unimplemented (0 hits for `@blend_src`) and no WebGPU-side capability predicate exists. WebGL2 has no dual-source blending — the documented SR-2 architectural-impossibility exception. | Chrome and Firefox 155 shipping `dual-source-blending` |
| **G5** | Close the WebGPU **snap** gates and port `surfacePosition`. The snap tier exists and its row is PARTIAL with the owed gates listed; `surfacePosition` is absent from `Scene/Snapping.js`. No new design — the Edge certification the fork already owes plus a small API port after G1, sequenced after the pick-readback measurement. | CesiumJS 1.144/1.145 `Scene.snap` |
| **G6** | Finish the WebGPU **voxel** path to upstream's default shader **before** adding the additive empty-space skip. The march preserves `stepSize` and shapes/padding are live, but the metadata-typed default CustomShader and GLSL user shaders are unrenderable and traversal stops at level 2. The audit adds only an ordering constraint and a cross-runtime oracle (cesium-native 0.56 renders the same extension). | upstream `VoxelPrimitive`; 3DTILES_content_voxels |
| **G7** | **GPU-driven traversal and draw generation for tiles.** The indirect-draw manager is scaffolded, the culler's INDIRECT mode zeroes `instanceCount` with the hint at `never`, `_drawCountBuffer` is CPU-written and never consumed, tile/voxel/splat selection is CPU-side, and the culler accepts exactly six planes. No new track — this is the meshlet Phase M3 generalised; the audit's contribution is an arbitrary-plane culling-volume input so the CPU and GPU cullers accept the same shape, and re-examining the packed-32-bit visbuffer trade-off before Tier-1 rows are briefed. | luma `GPUVirtualGeometrySelection` / `GPUSceneDrawGeneration`; three.js TSL rasterizer; cesium-native culling volumes |
| **G8** | The GPU **splat** path: compute sort first, residency behind the C18-S7 ruling. The renderer consumes the shared WASM radix-sort permutation and there is no GPU sort. A counting sort over 4096 depth bins is simpler than a full radix sort and fits the storage-buffer layout; WASM remains the WebGL and fallback path. Nothing here changes the gates. | `@luma.gl/splats`; three.js r186 four-pass counting sort |
| **G9** | A **frame graph** that owns pass order and transient lifetime. No graph exists; passes are hand-ordered, the attachment-demand registry is observational with `forceSceneMRT` true, and there are 51 `queue.submit(` sites in 34 files. Already the fork's ADR-4 / FAR-40x plan; luma's "compiled graph owns transients, borrows imports" and compile-once/encode-per-frame model are the reference. Ordering: observe-only compiler before any authority switch — **and a graph cannot land on 5,140-line files**, so it follows the DX decomposition. | luma `GPUCommandGraph`; three.js `RenderPipeline` fusion |
| **G10** | **One scene executor** over the backend boundary. The fork instantiates a 5,140-line `WebGPUSceneRenderer` as `_alternateSceneRenderer` beside `SceneRenderer.js`, 12 Scene files reference `isWebGPU`, and `GraphicsContext` covers frame/draw/clear/readback but not pipeline keys, shader generation or compute. Principle 2 taken to its conclusion; depends on G9, the DX decomposition and FAR-300 semantic packets. **Not dispatchable this wave** — recorded so the gap list is honest about where the field went. | three.js's single `Renderer` over an abstract `Backend` |

**Survey correction (2026-09-03, `1390-12 [shaderasync]`):** `G3` counts 13 `*_f16.wgsl` twins; there are **12** (`PostProcess/*_f16.wgsl`). The gap itself stands. **G1–G8 are queued as pointer rows** (`AR-G1..AR-G8`) in [`QUEUE_2026-09-03_ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW.md`](QUEUE_2026-09-03_ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW.md); the landscape census salvage that backs them is at `cesium-webgpu-worker-archive/arch-lenses-2026-09-03/landscape-salvage/`.

## 7. What this review did not cover

Stated plainly, because the gaps are the residual risk in archiving anything.

### 7.1 The 375 closed items, re-derived

Each judgement marked a set of items RESOLVED / SUPERSEDED / STALE — **440** in total. Verifiers re-derived **65** of them (57 spot-checks, three of which each adjudicated several ids), and five reversed. The remaining **375** were re-derived in batch L3 (2026-09-04) by four independent Opus readers plus an adversarial verifier, all read-only at HEAD `01226c648a`.

**Result: 365 STANDS-CLOSED, 10 REVERSED, 0 unadjudicated.** The ten reversals collapse to six distinct defects; one, `NEW-WEBGPU-BILLBOARD-ALIGNED-AXIS-UNREAD`, is a user-visible parity defect that has been shipping silently — `billboard.alignedAxis` is written to the WebGPU vertex stream and read by no shader.

**A second pass then exhausted the riskiest residual population.** Verification had found one closure upheld in error (`#85`), closed on a ledger heading with no code opened. All **110** STANDS-CLOSED rows whose evidence cites no engine source file — 89 of them resting on a ledger, queue or document path alone — were re-opened against the code: **109 stand, and the single reversal is `#85` itself.** No second instance exists. The residual risk in the closed set is now bounded by measurement rather than by sampling.

| Document | Closed | Re-derived by a verifier | Re-derived in L3 | Reversed |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| `AUDIT_2026_05_02` | 56 | 6 | 50 | 1 |
| `audits/2026-04-30_ARCHITECTURE_PERFORMANCE` | 32 | 7 | 25 | 0 |
| `audits/2026-04-30_FORK_FEATURE_INVENTORY` | 45 | 5 | 40 | 4 |
| `audits/2026-04-30_MAINTAINABILITY_SURVIVABILITY` | 22 | 12 | 10 | 1 |
| `PRINCIPAL_ENGINEER_REVIEW_RENDERER_DEEP` | 39 | 7 | 32 | 0 |
| `LOCAL_CHANGE_AUDIT_2026-07-31` | 46 | 5 | 41 | 0 |
| `FABLE5_PROGRESS_AND_ACTION_AUDIT` | 26 | 5 | 21 | 0 |
| `PRINCIPAL_ENGINEER_REVIEW_PER_FEATURE` | 56 | 5 | 51 | 0 |
| `FORK_VS_UPSTREAM…2026-07-13` | 17 | 7 | 10 | 1 |
| `audits/2026-06-11_ULTRA_REVIEW` | 101 | 6 | 95 | 3 |
| **Total** | **440** | **65** | **375** | **10** |

*(The earlier draft of this section reported 383 never-re-derived items. That figure counted spot-checks rather than items: three verifiers ran checks that each adjudicated several ids. The corrected derivation is in the L3 bank.)*

**What still rests on one reader.** The L3 verdicts are themselves single-reader for roughly 222 of the 375: adversarial verification covered the 10 reversals and 40 sampled STANDS-CLOSED, and the ledger-only sweep re-opened 110 rows (7 overlapping), so about 153 carry two L3 readers. `#85` — a single-reader STANDS-CLOSED that verification overturned — is the measured error rate of that residual, not a hypothetical.

**Archiving may now proceed on the record, not on trust.** The ten `*.judgement.json` files stay in the bank permanently and every closed item keeps its id, title, claim, status and evidence line; the L3 banks add a second reader's verdict at HEAD for all 375. Six rows must be opened first (L3 §3), and the `[GOVERNANCE-LOSS]` items must land in a live document before their source is retired — retiring a document that is the sole home of a rule is how `REJECT-4` was nearly lost.

**What the exercise showed about closure quality.** Ten live findings had been closed by assertion, and three of them share one shape: a credited fix whose *infrastructure* landed while its *behaviour* did not (`DP-H2`, Batch 117's fog docstring, the FAR-003 containment). Verification also overturned a reversal and a stands-closed in the same document — the first because two "independent" scripts shared a blind spot, the second because a reader closed a correctness item on a ledger heading without opening the code. **The failure mode is not carelessness; it is treating a ledger row as evidence of behaviour.** Future closures should cite a `packages/engine/Source` line or a proven commit, never a batch number or a queue path alone.

**The ledger has a matching authoring defect, and it is narrow enough to forbid.** The sweep shows the problem is not struck-through headings — four aggregates closed on them re-derived true — but rows whose *heading* asserts RESOLVED while their *body* records a containment. `#85`'s heading gave a batch id and a verdict; five lines below, its own body reads "Default OFF". The three features that are genuinely contained (`volumetricFog`, `C9-10` MRT, `_hiZConsumeEnabled`) produced no bad closure precisely because each states its containment in the label. A ledger row whose heading claims resolution while its body records a gate should be treated as malformed.

### 7.3 One sub-claim nobody could reach

`F5-09`'s allocation-rate claim — **"one bound function allocated per visible model per frame"** — was never measured when written and is not measured now. The rest of `F5-09` is adjudicated (§3.1). It needs a measurement, not a re-read, so no amount of document work closes it. Carried forward explicitly as **unverified**.

`HP-01`'s "dozens of unlabelled pass-creation sites" is a **runtime-coverage measurement**, not a source fact; the verifier re-derived only the repair-and-gating half (§3.9). Do not quote the figure without measuring it.

Each names the number the run must produce; the multi-metric rule applies (call counts, timings, memory and allocations together, noise behaviour stated beside each bar). Everything here is where the standing rule bites: "where a probe can measure the feature directly, the probe is the acceptance."

**2026-09-04 — the runtime leg has partly run, and what came back changes the shape of the problem.** Éowyn (bespoke tier-2 executor, job 2) banked receipts at `Tools/visual-regression/output/eowyn-job2-2026-09-02/`, `.../arch-review-runtime-2026-09-03/` and `.../wave-end/2026-09-02/`. Counted against the queue's `AR-M01`..`AR-M34` plus the three obligations §6 named without minting (Éowyn items 13 and 14, and `AR-049`'s owed item 15, now `AR-M35`..`AR-M37`), the population is **37 measurement obligations**, and the split is **4 RAN (one PARTIAL) / 33 NOT RUN** — not the "4 RAN, 30 NOT RUN" the job's own summary prose states over a 34-row table showing 3 numbers and 31 NOT RUNs. **The 33 did not fail; they were never instrumented.** Each names the instrument its receipt says is missing, and those 33 collapse into **ten instrument families** (per-backend capture pair, post-first-frame mutation-and-re-observe, pick/visibility matrix, per-frame cost-and-allocation profiler, encoder-wrapper census, GPU resource/write ledger, forced-gate pass timing, worker-pool and async lifecycle, malformed-document fixtures, in-page state/error read). **The bottleneck is instrument construction, not executor time**, so the queue carries one tool row per family rather than one per measurement.

What the four that ran produced: **Hi-Z dispatches = 0 in all 27 samples** at 3,600 and 10,000 DrawCommands, so neither producer nor consumer runs and §4 `R1` is now contradicted by measurement as well as by code; **the sun disc at 0.32x WebGL** with parity 0.0233 against a 0.02 bar; **GPU-timestamp coverage 0.40-0.56 over three named passes**, which is the number `HP-01` needed and §7.4 refused to let anyone quote unmeasured; and the first-ever `npm run test-webgpu` receipt for `ARCH-8` — **18,236 of 18,318 specs executed, 95 failed across 38 suites, 82 skipped, 1 error, 9 m 40 s**, with the hardware WebGPU lane 10/10. Two GPU **validation faults** surfaced from the Sandcastle2 sweep on shipped demos (`elevation-band-material`, `frustum-dev`) and are queued P0. The visual-regression baselines were found **structurally incomplete on `main`** — five scenes with no baseline, `high-density-5k-spheres` baselined on black frames, only 4 of 10 manifest-listed — and `globe-default`'s historical 91.3 % mismatch is a **uniform darkening on both backends** (meanLum 101.8 -> 72.6), i.e. a real scene change since the baseline and not a WebGPU defect. Rows: `AR-M35`..`AR-M37` and the instrument-family tool rows in the queue.
